--- STEM-Matrix-main/README.md ---

# StemMatrix: On-Device Neuro-Symbolic SLM

> An offline, zero-cloud-cost learning platform for STEM students. Ask a concept question and get a textbook-grounded answer with the book cited. Ask a numerical problem and get an exact, solver-verified answer. Every part of every answer is labelled with where it came from: **Textbook**, **Solver** or **Model**.

![Status](https://img.shields.io/badge/status-in%20development-orange)
![Frontend](https://img.shields.io/badge/frontend-Next.js-black)
![Backend](https://img.shields.io/badge/backend-FastAPI-009688)
![Model](https://img.shields.io/badge/model-SLM%20%2B%20QLoRA-blue)
![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-MIT-green)

B.Tech Major Project, Department of Computer Science & Engineering, Islamic University of Science and Technology (IUST), Awantipora, Jammu & Kashmir, India. 2026.

## Why StemMatrix

STEM learning needs two different things: **trustworthy explanations** of concepts and formulas, and **exact answers** to numerical problems. Cloud-hosted LLM study tools struggle with both:

- **Cost and connectivity.** They need per-token API payments and a constant internet connection.
- **Privacy.** Student data leaves the device.
- **Unreliable facts.** Models can state formulas and definitions confidently with no source, and students cannot tell what to trust.
- **Unreliable arithmetic.** Autoregressive models predict likely tokens rather than computing, so they make silent errors in numerical work. Asking a model to check itself without external feedback rarely helps.

StemMatrix addresses these by running everything on the device and being explicit about provenance:

1. A **fine-tuned small language model (SLM)** explains and reasons step by step.
2. **Hybrid retrieval** over standard offline textbooks grounds explanations and formula lookups, with the book, chapter and page cited.
3. A **neuro-symbolic layer** of deterministic solvers computes exact numerical answers and verifies the model's output.
4. A **self-healing loop** feeds solver errors back to the model so it can repair its own reasoning.
5. A **source label** on every part of the answer shows whether it came from a textbook, a solver or the model alone.

## Key Features

- **Fully offline.** The model, retrieval index and solvers all run locally.
- **Textbook Q&A with citations.** Ask "What is Lenz's law?" or "How do I integrate sin x?" and get the answer from a standard textbook, with the book, chapter, section and page.
- **Clear source labels.** Each part of an answer is marked as Textbook (RAG), Solver (verified) or Model (LLM, no source). If nothing relevant is found in the books, the app says so rather than guessing silently.
- **Exact answers.** Numerical results come from verified solvers (SymPy, NumPy, SciPy, Pint), not from token prediction.
- **Self-healing reasoning.** When the model's answer disagrees with the solver, the error is sent back and the model retries.
- **Hybrid retrieval.** Dense embeddings and BM25 keyword search, merged with Reciprocal Rank Fusion, find exact technical terms that vector-only search misses.
- **Interactive learning.** Follow-up questions, click-through citations that open the source passage, practice questions with several formats, and flashcards for formulas.
- **Transparent UI.** A verification panel shows every solver attempt (model answer, solver answer, mismatch, repair), with math rendered in LaTeX.

## Two Modes, One Pipeline

A router classifies each question and sends it down the right path. Both paths share the same retrieval layer and the same model.

| Mode | Example | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| **Textbook Q&A** | "What are electric fields?", "State Lenz's law", "Formula for integration by parts" | Hybrid retrieval pulls passages from the textbooks; the SLM writes a brief answer grounded in them, with citations |
| **Problem solving** | "Integrate x^2 from 0 to 3", "Find the eigenvalues of this matrix" | The SLM reasons step by step and emits structured parameters; a solver computes the exact answer and verifies the model; mismatches trigger the repair loop |
| **Out of scope** | Anything outside the supported subjects | Polite refusal |

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    Q["Student question"] --> R{"Router"}
    R -->|"Out of scope"| X["Polite refusal"]
    R -->|"Textbook QA"| RET["Hybrid retrieval: BM25 + dense + RRF"]
    R -->|"Problem solving"| CTX["Retrieve formulas for context (optional)"]
    RET --> CONF{"Relevant passages found?"}
    CONF -->|"No"| NOSRC["Answer from model only, labelled Model"]
    CONF -->|"Yes"| GEN["SLM writes grounded answer with citation markers"]
    GEN --> CHECK["Backend validates citations against retrieved passages"]
    CHECK --> OUT["Answer with source labels and book references"]
    CTX --> SLM["SLM reasoning + structured JSON"]
    SLM --> SOL["Deterministic solver"]
    SOL --> V{"Answers match?"}
    V -->|"Yes"| A["Verified answer + steps + sources"]
    V -->|"No, attempts left"| FB["Error feedback to SLM"]
    FB --> SLM
    V -->|"No, attempts exhausted"| FALL["Return solver answer with a note"]
```

## Source Labels and Citations

Every answer is returned as a list of segments, and each segment carries its origin. The frontend renders each origin with a distinct badge.

| Label | Meaning | Shown with |
|---|---|---|
| **Textbook (RAG)** | The statement is supported by a retrieved textbook passage | Book title, chapter, section, page; click to open the passage |
| **Solver (verified)** | The number or result was computed by a deterministic solver | Solver name and the computation trace |
| **Model (LLM)** | Generated by the language model with no supporting source | A visible "not from a source" notice |

**Labels are assigned by the backend, not by the model.** The SLM is prompted to attach citation markers like `[1]` to claims. The backend then checks that each marker points to a passage that was actually retrieved, and labels any sentence without a valid citation as Model. This prevents the model from claiming a source it did not use.

A segment looks roughly like this (final schema may differ):

```json
{
  "text": "The induced emf opposes the change in magnetic flux that produces it.",
  "source_type": "textbook",
  "citations": [
    {
      "book": "University Physics Volume 2",
      "chapter": "Electromagnetic Induction",
      "section": "Lenz's Law",
      "page": 0,
      "chunk_id": "up2-ch13-s04-c02"
    }
  ]
}
```

*(Page and chapter values above are placeholders; real values come from the ingested corpus metadata.)*

**Low-confidence retrieval.** If the best retrieval score is below a threshold, the app says that nothing relevant was found in the textbooks and clearly labels the answer as coming from the model alone.

## Question and Answer Types

The app is interactive and supports multiple question and answer formats.

| Type | Description | Verified by |
|---|---|---|
| **Concept explanation** | Brief explanations ("What is a magnetic field?") | Textbook citations |
| **Formula lookup** | Standard formulas and rules, shown as formula cards ("integral of sin x") | Textbook citations |
| **Worked numerical problem** | Step-by-step solution with an exact final answer | Solver + self-healing loop |
| **Multiple choice (MCQ)** | One correct option; explanation with source | Solver where numeric, textbook otherwise |
| **Multiple select (MSQ)** | One or more correct options | Textbook / solver |
| **Numeric answer (NAT)** | Type in a number and get graded against the solver's exact value | Solver |
| **Short answer / derivation** | Free-text answers with step-by-step reasoning | Textbook citations |
| **Practice mode** | Generate practice questions from a chosen chapter and check your answers | Solver + textbook |
| **Flashcards** | Formulas and definitions pulled from the textbooks | Textbook citations |

**Interactive features**
- Follow-up questions in the same chat ("explain that more simply", "show an example").
- Click a citation to open the source passage in a side panel.
- Filter by subject or by book.
- Thumbs up/down feedback on answers, logged for later fine-tuning and evaluation.
- Chat history.

## How the Self-Healing Loop Works

For numerical problems, the SLM emits structured parameters (for example `{"type": "definite_integral", "expr": "x**2", "lower": 0, "upper": 3}`) alongside its reasoning, rather than relying on parsing free text. The solver computes the exact result from that JSON and compares it with the model's final answer. On a mismatch, the solver's output is sent back to the model as feedback and the model tries again, up to a fixed number of attempts. If it still fails, the solver's answer is returned with a note.

**Self-generated training data.** The same solvers produce training data: random problems with exact answers and step traces, used to fine-tune the SLM with QLoRA.

## Textbook Corpus

The initial corpus uses openly licensed textbooks so they can be redistributed and indexed freely:

| Subject | Books |
|---|---|
| Engineering mathematics | OpenStax Calculus (Volumes 1-3), and other openly licensed texts for linear algebra and probability |
| Physics | OpenStax University Physics (Volumes 1-3) |

**Ingestion**
- Where available, prefer the source HTML or other structured formats over PDF extraction, since they preserve equations far better.
- Chunk by section headings, and keep formula blocks together with their surrounding text.
- Store metadata with every chunk: book, chapter, section, page, chunk ID.
- Index each chunk twice: a BM25 keyword index and a dense embedding index.

> Do not index commercial textbooks you do not have the right to redistribute. The architecture works with any text, but the repository should only ship openly licensed material.

## Tech Stack

| Layer | Technology |
|---|---|
| Frontend | Next.js (TypeScript), Tailwind CSS, shadcn/ui, KaTeX |
| Backend | Python, FastAPI, Pydantic, SQLite |
| Model | Small open model (Qwen2.5 0.5B-1.5B class), QLoRA fine-tuning (`peft`, `bitsandbytes`), GGUF 4-bit quantization |
| Inference | llama.cpp / Ollama |
| Retrieval | `rank_bm25`, `sentence-transformers`, Chroma or FAISS, Reciprocal Rank Fusion |
| Solvers | SymPy, NumPy, SciPy, Pint |
| Evaluation | RAGAS plus a custom held-out test set |

> The initial subject focus is engineering mathematics and physics (calculus, linear algebra, probability and statistics, mechanics, electricity and magnetism). The architecture is subject-agnostic: a new subject needs a corpus and a set of solvers.

## Repository Structure

> Planned layout. Update as the project evolves.

```
stemmatrix/
├── frontend/              # Next.js app (chat UI, source labels, verification panel, practice mode)
├── backend/
│   ├── app/
│   │   ├── api/           # FastAPI routes, streaming endpoints
│   │   ├── orchestrator/  # router, pipeline, self-healing loop
│   │   ├── retrieval/     # BM25 + dense + RRF, citation validation
│   │   ├── solvers/       # deterministic solver modules
│   │   ├── practice/      # question generation, grading, flashcards
│   │   ├── schemas/       # Pydantic models, answer segments, SLM-to-solver JSON schema
│   │   └── db/            # SQLite models, attempt logging, feedback
│   └── tests/
├── finetune/              # data generation, QLoRA training, GGUF export
├── corpus/                # openly licensed books, metadata, ingestion scripts
├── eval/                  # test sets, baselines, evaluation scripts
└── docs/                  # synopsis, report, architecture notes
```

## Getting Started

> Commands below describe the intended workflow. Adjust paths and names to match the final code.

### Prerequisites

- Python 3.10+
- Node.js 18+
- [Ollama](https://ollama.com) or llama.cpp for local inference
- A GPU is only needed for fine-tuning (free Colab/Kaggle tiers are enough); inference runs on CPU

### 1. Clone

```bash
git clone https://github.com/<your-org>/stemmatrix.git
cd stemmatrix
```

### 2. Backend

```bash
cd backend
python -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate        # Windows: .venv\Scripts\activate
pip install -r requirements.txt
cp .env.example .env             # configure model endpoint, paths
uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000
```

### 3. Model

Place the fine-tuned GGUF model in `models/` and register it with Ollama:

```bash
ollama create stemmatrix -f models/Modelfile
```

### 4. Build the textbook index

Add the openly licensed books to `corpus/books/`, then:

```bash
python -m app.retrieval.ingest --corpus ../corpus
```

### 5. Frontend

```bash
cd frontend
npm install
npm run dev                      # http://localhost:3000
```

### Run tests

```bash
cd backend && pytest
```

## API Overview

The FastAPI backend publishes an OpenAPI spec at `http://localhost:8000/docs`. TypeScript types for the frontend are generated from it.

| Endpoint | Method | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `/api/chat` | POST (SSE) | Submit a question; streams answer segments (with source labels), solver attempts and the final answer |
| `/api/sources/{chunk_id}` | GET | Fetch a cited textbook passage with its book, chapter, section and page |
| `/api/books` | GET | List indexed books and subjects |
| `/api/practice` | POST | Generate practice questions (MCQ, MSQ, NAT, short answer) from a chapter |
| `/api/practice/grade` | POST | Grade a submitted answer |
| `/api/feedback` | POST | Record thumbs up/down on an answer |
| `/api/sessions` | GET | List past chat sessions |
| `/api/sessions/{id}` | GET | Fetch a session with its attempts and sources |
| `/api/health` | GET | Backend and model server status |

*The routes above are planned and may change as the project develops.*

## Evaluation Plan

We evaluate the two modes separately on held-out question sets that were not used in training.

**Problem solving (about 80-100 questions)** across four configurations:

1. Base SLM (no fine-tuning, no retrieval)
2. Base SLM + retrieval
3. Fine-tuned SLM + retrieval
4. Full StemMatrix (fine-tuned SLM + retrieval + solvers + self-healing loop)

Metrics: final-answer accuracy, number of repair attempts needed, latency.

**Textbook Q&A (a hand-built set of questions mapped to the correct book sections)**

Metrics:
- **Retrieval quality:** whether the correct section appears in the top results (recall@k), comparing BM25 only, dense only and hybrid with RRF.
- **Citation accuracy:** whether the cited passage actually supports the claim it is attached to (checked by hand on a sample).
- **Faithfulness and context precision/recall:** RAGAS scores.
- **Source-label correctness:** whether segments without real support are correctly labelled as Model.

Results will be added here once the evaluation is complete.

## Roadmap

- [ ] Repository, API contract, answer-segment schema and SLM-to-solver JSON schema

- [ ] Demo and final report

--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_practice.py ---

from app.practice.service import PracticeService
from app.schemas.models import PracticeGenerateRequest, PracticeGradeRequest


def test_generates_practice_questions():
    result = PracticeService().generate(PracticeGenerateRequest(count=2))
    assert len(result.questions) == 2


def test_grades_correct_answer():
    result = PracticeService().grade(PracticeGradeRequest(question_id="integral-1", submitted_value=2.0))
    assert result.correct


def test_grades_wrong_answer():
    result = PracticeService().grade(PracticeGradeRequest(question_id="integral-1", submitted_value=3.0))
    assert not result.correct


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_input_notation.py ---

import pytest
from app.inputs.notation import (NotationError, normalize_chemical_equation, normalize_chemical_formula,
                                 normalize_expression, parse_plain_number, split_equation, split_number_prefix)
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.dispatcher import SolverDispatcher


def run(solver, **kw):
    return SolverDispatcher().dispatch(SolverRequest(request_id="n", solver=solver, **kw))


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text", ["x²", "x^2", "x**2", "x ^ 2", "x²"])
def test_power_notations_are_equivalent(text):
    assert normalize_expression(text, ["x"]) == "x**2"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text", ["x² − 5x + 6 = 0", "x^2 - 5x + 6 = 0", "x**2 - 5*x + 6 = 0", "x² − 5·x + 6 ＝ 0", "x^2-5x+6==0"])
def test_equivalent_equations_give_same_solutions(text):
    r = run("solve_equation", expression=text)
    assert r.status == "success" and set(r.result) == {"2", "3"} and r.verification.passed


def test_normalization_is_idempotent():
    for t in ["2x²−5x", "√(x+1)", "πr²", "3xy", "sin(x)cos(x)", "1.5×10⁻³·x", "|x|", "x⁻¹", "log10(x)"]:
        once = normalize_expression(t, ["x", "y", "r"])
        assert normalize_expression(once, ["x", "y", "r"]) == once


def test_multiplication_and_minus_signs():
    assert normalize_expression("3×4÷2−1") == "3*4/2-1"
    assert normalize_expression("a·b", ["a", "b"]) == "a*b"


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text,value", [("1e-5", 1e-5), ("1E−5", 1e-5), ("1.5×10⁻³", 1.5e-3), ("6.022×10^23", 6.022e23),
                                        ("2.5e+3", 2500.0), ("1,000", 1000.0), ("−3", -3.0)])
def test_scientific_notation(text, value):
    assert parse_plain_number(text) == pytest.approx(value)


def test_scientific_notation_in_expressions_is_not_euler():
    assert run("evaluate", expression="1E−5").numeric_result == pytest.approx(1e-5)       # was -2.28 (1*E - 5)
    assert run("evaluate", expression="2.5e3 + 1").numeric_result == pytest.approx(2501)
    assert run("evaluate", expression="1.5×10⁻³").numeric_result == pytest.approx(1.5e-3)


def test_function_and_variable_names_are_preserved():
    assert normalize_expression("log10(x)", ["x"]) == "log10(x)"
    assert normalize_expression("x1 + 1", ["x1"]) == "x1+1"
    assert normalize_expression("arcsin(x)", ["x"]) == "asin(x)"
    assert run("evaluate", expression="log10(1000)").numeric_result == pytest.approx(3)
    r = run("evaluate", expression="x1 + 1", variable="x1", extra_params={"substitutions": {"x1": 2}})
    assert r.numeric_result == 3


def test_square_root_sign_and_abs_bars():
    assert run("evaluate", expression="√16 + |−3|").numeric_result == 7


def test_implicit_multiplication():
    r = run("evaluate", expression="2x(x+1)", extra_params={"substitutions": {"x": 2}})
    assert r.numeric_result == 12
    assert normalize_expression("3xy", ["x", "y"]) == "3*x*y"


def test_equation_boundaries():
    assert split_equation("2x == 4", ["x"]) == ("2*x", "4")
    assert split_equation("x + 1") == ("x+1", "0")
    with pytest.raises(NotationError):
        split_equation("x = 1 = 2")
    with pytest.raises(NotationError):
        split_equation("x < 3")
    with pytest.raises(NotationError):
        split_equation("= 3")


def test_chemical_formulas_are_preserved_not_treated_as_maths():
    assert normalize_chemical_formula("H₂O") == "H2O"
    assert normalize_chemical_formula("CuSO₄·5H₂O") == "CuSO4.5H2O"
    assert normalize_chemical_formula("Co") == "Co" and normalize_chemical_formula("CO") == "CO"
    left, right, notes = normalize_chemical_equation("2H₂(g) + O₂(g) ⇌ 2H₂O(l)")
    assert (left, right) == (["H2", "O2"], ["H2O"])
    for arrow in ["->", "-->", "=>", "→", "⟶", "="]:
        assert normalize_chemical_equation(f"H2 + O2 {arrow} H2O")[1] == ["H2O"]
    with pytest.raises(NotationError):
        normalize_chemical_equation("H2 + O2")


def test_chemistry_solvers_use_shared_notation():
    r = run("chemistry_molar_mass", extra_params={"formula": "KMnO₄"})
    assert r.status == "success" and r.result["molar_mass_g_mol"] == pytest.approx(158.03, abs=0.01)
    r = run("chemistry_molar_mass", extra_params={"formula": "CuSO4·5H2O"})
    assert r.result["molar_mass_g_mol"] == pytest.approx(249.68, abs=0.01)
    r = run("chemistry_balance_equation", extra_params={"equation": "H2(g) + O2(g) => H2O(l)"})
    assert r.result["balanced_equation"] == "2H2 + O2 -> 2H2O"
    r = run("chemistry_balance_equation", extra_params={"equation": "H2 + O2 -> H2O + H2O2"})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "more than one" in r.error


def test_number_prefix_rejects_decimal_comma():
    with pytest.raises(NotationError):
        split_number_prefix("2,5 kg")
    assert split_number_prefix("1,000 g") == (1000.0, "g")


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_input_units.py ---

import pytest
from app.inputs.units import (ACCELERATION, FORCE, MASS, DimensionError, UnitError, convert, parse_quantity, parse_unit)
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.dispatcher import SolverDispatcher


def run(solver, **kw):
    return SolverDispatcher().dispatch(SolverRequest(request_id="u", solver=solver, **kw))


@pytest.mark.parametrize("text,dim,si", [
    ("500 g", MASS, 0.5), ("500g", MASS, 0.5), ("2 m/s²", ACCELERATION, 2), ("2 m/s^2", ACCELERATION, 2),
    ("2 m/s**2", ACCELERATION, 2), ("9.81 m s^-2", ACCELERATION, 9.81), ("1 kg*m/s^2", FORCE, 1), ("1 N", FORCE, 1),
    ("1.5×10⁻³ kg", MASS, 1.5e-3), ("1 lb", MASS, 0.45359237)])
def test_parse_quantity(text, dim, si):
    q = parse_quantity(text)
    assert q.dimension == dim and q.to_si() == pytest.approx(si)


def test_temperatures():
    assert parse_quantity("25 °C").to_si() == pytest.approx(298.15)
    assert parse_quantity("25°C").to_si() == pytest.approx(298.15)
    assert parse_quantity("98.6 °F").to_si() == pytest.approx(310.15)
    assert convert(100, "°C", "°F") == pytest.approx(212)
    assert convert(-40, "°C", "°F") == pytest.approx(-40)
    assert convert(25, "°C", "K", delta=True) == pytest.approx(25)       # an interval, not a point
    # inside a compound unit the degree is an interval
    assert parse_quantity("1 cal/(g·°C)").to("J/(kg*K)") == pytest.approx(4184)


def test_si_prefix_case_is_respected():
    assert convert(1, "MV", "V") == 1e6 and convert(1, "mV", "V") == 1e-3
    assert convert(1, "Mm", "m") == 1e6 and convert(1, "mm", "m") == 1e-3
    assert convert(1, "mv", "V") == pytest.approx(1e-3)        # lower-case prefix means milli
    notes = []
    parse_unit("Kg", notes=notes)
    assert notes and "kg" in notes[0]                           # non-exact readings are reported


def test_molar_is_not_meter():
    assert convert(1, "M", "mol/m^3") == 1000
    with pytest.raises(DimensionError):
        convert(1, "M", "m")


def test_incompatible_dimensions_are_rejected():
    with pytest.raises(DimensionError, match="Incompatible units"):
        convert(1, "kg", "m")
    with pytest.raises(UnitError):
        parse_unit("furlongs")


def test_unit_convert_solver_and_legacy_names():
    assert run("unit_convert", extra_params={"value": 100, "from_unit": "km/h", "to_unit": "m/s"}).result["value"] == pytest.approx(27.7778, abs=1e-3)
    assert run("unit_convert", extra_params={"value": 0, "from_unit": "celsius", "to_unit": "fahrenheit"}).result["value"] == pytest.approx(32)
    assert run("unit_convert", extra_params={"value": 1, "from_unit": "MV", "to_unit": "V"}).result["value"] == 1e6
    r = run("unit_convert", extra_params={"value": "100 km/h", "to_unit": "m/s"})
    assert r.status == "success"
    r = run("unit_convert", extra_params={"value": 1, "from_unit": "kg", "to_unit": "m"})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "Incompatible units" in r.error
    r = run("unit_convert", extra_params={"value": 1, "from_unit": "kg"})
    assert r.status == "failed" and r.details["preflight"]["issues"][0]["category"] == "missing"


def test_quantities_are_converted_to_solver_units():
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": "500 g", "acceleration": "2 m/s²"})
    assert r.status == "success" and r.numeric_result == pytest.approx(1.0)
    assert r.details["preflight"]["conversions"][0]["field"] == "mass"
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": {"value": 2, "unit": "lb"}, "acceleration": 1})
    assert r.numeric_result == pytest.approx(0.90718474)
    r = run("physics_ideal_gas", extra_params={"pressure": "1 atm", "volume": "22.41396954 L", "moles": 1, "temperature": "0 °C"})
    assert r.status == "success" and r.verification.passed                      # 0 °C -> 273.15 K


def test_temperature_change_is_an_interval():
    r = run("physics_heat_energy", extra_params={"mass": "1 kg", "specific_heat": "4.184 kJ/(kg·K)", "temperature_change": "25 °C"})
    assert r.numeric_result == pytest.approx(104600)            # NOT 25 °C -> 298 K


def test_incompatible_quantity_is_rejected_not_computed():
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": "5 m", "acceleration": 2})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "dimension" in r.error and r.details["preflight"]["issues"][0]["code"] == "dimension_mismatch"
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": 5, "acceleration": "2 kg"})
    assert r.status == "failed"
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": "5 furlongs", "acceleration": 2})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "Unsupported unit" in r.error


def test_expected_unit_converts_result_and_checks_dimension():
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": 1000, "acceleration": 1, "expected_unit": "kN"})
    assert r.details["preflight"]["converted_result"] == {"value": pytest.approx(1.0), "unit": "kN"}
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": 1, "acceleration": 1, "expected_unit": "J"})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "expected_unit" in r.error


def test_mixed_unit_and_plain_ratio_is_rejected():
    r = run("engineering_safety_factor", extra_params={"failure_strength": "300 MPa", "working_stress": 100})
    assert r.status == "failed"
    r = run("engineering_safety_factor", extra_params={"failure_strength": "300 MPa", "working_stress": "100 MPa"})
    assert r.numeric_result == pytest.approx(3)

--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_core_math.py ---

import math
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.dispatcher import SolverDispatcher


def run(solver, **kwargs):
    payload = {"request_id": "test", "solver": solver, **kwargs}
    return SolverDispatcher().dispatch(SolverRequest(**payload))


def test_arithmetic_and_symbolic_foundations():
    assert run("evaluate", expression="(3/4)+(5/6)").numeric_result == 19 / 12
    assert run("simplify", expression="(x**2 - 1)/(x - 1)").result == "x + 1"
    assert run("factor", expression="x**2 - 5*x + 6").result == "(x - 3)*(x - 2)"


def test_algebra_roots_and_systems():
    result = run("solve_equation", expression="2*x+5=15")
    assert result.status == "success" and result.result == ["5"]
    result = run("polynomial_roots", expression="x**2-5*x+6")
    assert set(result.result) == {"2", "3"}
    result = run("solve_system", equations=["x+y=5", "x-y=1"], variables=["x", "y"])
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert result.result == [{"x": "3", "y": "2"}]


def test_calculus():
    assert run("differentiate", expression="x**3+2*x**2").result == "3*x**2 + 4*x"
    assert run("indefinite_integral", expression="x**2").verification.passed
    result = run("definite_integral", expression="x**2", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3)
    assert result.numeric_result == 9


def test_linear_algebra():
    result = run("solve_linear_system", matrix=[[1, 1], [1, -1]], vector=[5, 1])
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert result.result == [3.0, 2.0]
    assert result.verification.passed
    assert abs(run("matrix_determinant", matrix=[[1, 2], [3, 4]]).numeric_result + 2) < 1e-12
    assert run("vector_dot", values=[1, 2, 3], extra_params={"vector_b": [4, 5, 6]}).numeric_result == 32


def test_numerical_methods():
    result = run("bisection_root", expression="x**2-2", lower_bound=1, upper_bound=2)
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert abs(result.numeric_result - math.sqrt(2)) < 1e-6
    result = run("simpson_integral", expression="x**2", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3)
    assert abs(result.numeric_result - 9) < 1e-8
    result = run("interpolate", extra_params={"x_values": [0, 1, 2], "y_values": [1, 3, 5], "at": 3})
    assert abs(result.numeric_result - 7) < 1e-8


def test_statistics_and_probability():
    result = run("descriptive_stats", data=[1, 2, 2, 3, 4])
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert result.result["mean"] == 2.4
    assert result.result["median"] == 2
    assert run("binomial_probability", extra_params={"n": 4, "k": 2, "p": 0.5}).numeric_result == 0.375
    assert abs(run("normal_probability", extra_params={"x": 0, "mean": 0, "standard_deviation": 1}).numeric_result - 0.5) < 1e-12
    assert abs(run("correlation", extra_params={"x_values": [1, 2, 3], "y_values": [2, 4, 6]}).numeric_result - 1) < 1e-12


def test_rejects_unsafe_expression():
    result = run("evaluate", expression="__import__('os').system('echo unsafe')")
    assert result.status == "failed"


def test_equation_solver_accepts_full_equations_and_common_notation():
    # The exact user-reported request must work, not only the expression-only form.
    result = run(
        "solve_equation",
        expression="x**2 - 5*x + 6 = 0",
        variable="x",
    )
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert set(result.result) == {"2", "3"}

    # Common math notation should normalize to the same equation.
    result = run(
        "solve_equation",
        expression="x^2 − 5×x + 6 = 0",
        variable="x",
    )
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert set(result.result) == {"2", "3"}


def test_newton_root_works_with_initial_guess_and_no_bounds():
    result = run(
        "newton_root",
        expression="x**2 - 2",
        variable="x",
        initial_guess=1.5,
        tolerance=1e-8,
        max_iterations=100,
    )
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert abs(result.numeric_result - math.sqrt(2)) < 1e-7
    assert result.details["method"] == "newton"


def test_newton_root_requires_initial_guess_not_bounds():
    result = run("newton_root", expression="x**2 - 2", variable="x")
    assert result.status == "failed"
    assert "initial_guess" in result.error


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_orchestrator.py ---

from app.orchestrator.router import QuestionRouter
from app.orchestrator.self_healing import SelfHealingSolver
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.dispatcher import SolverDispatcher


def test_router_selects_problem_solving():
    assert QuestionRouter().route("Integrate x**2 from 0 to 3").mode == "problem_solving"


def test_router_defaults_conceptual_question_to_textbook():
    assert QuestionRouter().route("What is an electric field?").mode == "textbook_qa"


def test_self_healing_marks_matching_answer_verified():
    req = SolverRequest(request_id="a", solver="definite_integral", expression="x**2", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3)
    result = SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher()).solve(req, initial_model_answer="9")
    assert result.status == "verified"


def test_self_healing_does_not_claim_model_match_when_wrong():
    req = SolverRequest(request_id="b", solver="definite_integral", expression="x**2", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3)
    result = SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher(), max_attempts=0).solve(req, initial_model_answer="12")
    assert result.status == "solver_result_unmatched"
    assert result.solver_result.exact_result == "9"


def test_self_healing_retries_with_mock_and_verifies_corrected_answer():
    from app.orchestrator.model_client import ScriptedMockModelClient

    req = SolverRequest(
        request_id="repair-1",
        solver="definite_integral",
        expression="x**2",
        lower_bound=0,
        upper_bound=3,
    )
    mock = ScriptedMockModelClient(["9"])
    result = SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher(), model_client=mock, max_attempts=2).solve(
        req, initial_model_answer="8"
    )

    assert result.status == "verified"
    assert len(result.attempts) == 2
    assert result.attempts[0].matched is False
    assert result.attempts[1].matched is True
    assert len(mock.calls) == 1
    assert mock.calls[0]["feedback"] is not None
    assert "9" in mock.calls[0]["feedback"]


def test_self_healing_stops_when_mock_answers_remain_wrong():
    from app.orchestrator.model_client import ScriptedMockModelClient

    req = SolverRequest(
        request_id="repair-2",
        solver="definite_integral",
        expression="x**2",
        lower_bound=0,
        upper_bound=3,
    )
    mock = ScriptedMockModelClient(["7", "8"])
    result = SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher(), model_client=mock, max_attempts=2).solve(
        req, initial_model_answer="6"
    )

    assert result.status == "solver_result_unmatched"
    assert len(result.attempts) == 3  # initial answer + two repair attempts
    assert all(not attempt.matched for attempt in result.attempts)
    assert result.solver_result.exact_result == "9"


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_loopholes.py ---

"""Regression tests for problems found in the solver audit."""
import os
import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient
from app.api.main import app
from app.orchestrator.model_client import ScriptedMockModelClient
from app.orchestrator.self_healing import SelfHealingSolver, answers_match, parse_numeric_answer
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.dispatcher import SolverDispatcher
from app.solvers.domain_stem import interpret_question
from app.solvers.registry import SolverRegistry


def run(solver, **kw):
    return SolverDispatcher().dispatch(SolverRequest(request_id="l", solver=solver, **kw))


@pytest.mark.parametrize("op,kw", [
    ("limit", dict(expression="x", extra_params={"point": "__import__('os').system('echo pwned > {p}')"})),
    ("taylor_series", dict(expression="x", extra_params={"point": "__import__('os').system('echo pwned > {p}')"})),
    ("interpolate", dict(extra_params={"x_values": [0, 1], "y_values": [0, 1], "at": "__import__('os').system('echo pwned > {p}')"})),
    ("interpolate", dict(extra_params={"x_values": ["__import__('os').system('echo pwned > {p}')", 1], "y_values": [0, 1], "at": 0.5})),
])
def test_no_code_execution_via_sympify(tmp_path, op, kw):
    marker = tmp_path / "pwned"
    payload = str(kw).replace("{p}", str(marker))
    kw = eval(payload)  # noqa: S307 - test-built literal dict only
    r = run(op, **kw)
    assert r.status == "failed" and not marker.exists()


def test_limit_and_series_still_work():
    assert run("limit", expression="sin(x)/x", extra_params={"point": 0}).result == "1"
    assert run("limit", expression="1/x", extra_params={"point": "oo"}).result == "0"
    assert run("limit", expression="sin(x)/x", extra_params={"point": "0", "direction": "+"}).result == "1"
    assert run("taylor_series", expression="exp(x)", degree=2, extra_params={"point": 0}).result.startswith("x**2/2")
    assert run("interpolate", extra_params={"x_values": [0, 1, 2], "y_values": [0, 1, 4], "at": 3}).numeric_result == pytest.approx(9)


def test_undefined_results_are_failures_not_successes():
    for expr in ["1/0", "tan(pi/2)", "log(0)"]:
        r = run("evaluate", expression=expr)
        assert r.status == "failed", expr


def test_resource_limits():
    assert run("evaluate", expression="factorial(300000)").status == "failed"
    assert run("evaluate", expression="((9**99)**99)**9").status == "failed"
    assert run("evaluate", expression="2**100").exact_result == str(2 ** 100)
    assert run("evaluate", expression="1e-300*1e-10").status == "success"


def test_trapezoid_and_simpson_of_constant():
    assert run("trapezoid_integral", expression="5", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=2).numeric_result == pytest.approx(10)
    assert run("simpson_integral", expression="x^2", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3).numeric_result == pytest.approx(9)


def test_definite_integral_accepts_common_notation_via_preflight():
    r = run("definite_integral", expression="x^2", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3)
    assert r.status == "success" and r.exact_result == "9"
    r = run("definite_integral", expression="x²", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3)
    assert r.exact_result == "9"
    assert run("definite_integral", expression="x**2", lower_bound=3, upper_bound=0).status == "failed"
    assert "Lower bound" in run("definite_integral", expression="x**2", lower_bound=3, upper_bound=0).error


def test_every_registered_solver_is_preflighted():
    reg = SolverRegistry()
    for name in reg.names():
        assert type(reg.get(name)).__name__ == "PreflightSolver", name

    class Echo:
        name = "echo"
        seen = None

        def solve(self, request):
            Echo.seen = request
            from app.schemas.models import SolverResponse
            return SolverResponse(request_id=request.request_id, status="success", solver="echo", result="ok")

    custom = SolverRegistry([Echo()])
    assert type(custom.get("echo")).__name__ == "PreflightSolver"


def test_solver_not_called_when_preflight_fails(monkeypatch):
    from app.solvers import domain_stem
    called = []
    monkeypatch.setattr(domain_stem.DomainSTEMSolver, "solve", lambda self, r: called.append(1))
    r = SolverDispatcher().dispatch(SolverRequest(request_id="x", solver="physics_force", extra_params={"mass": "5 m", "acceleration": 1}))
    assert r.status == "failed" and not called


# ---------------- self-healing ------------------------------------------------
def test_answer_matching():
    assert answers_match("9", 9.0) and answers_match("9.0", 9.0) and answers_match("9e0", 9.0)
    assert answers_match("1/3", 1 / 3) and answers_match("sqrt(2)/2", 2 ** 0.5 / 2) and answers_match("−3", -3.0)
    assert not answers_match("0", 1e-9)                 # a wrong zero no longer matches a tiny value
    assert not answers_match("abc", 1.0) and not answers_match(None, 1.0)
    assert not answers_match("__import__('os')", 1.0) and not answers_match("x+1", 1.0)
    assert parse_numeric_answer("9 N") is None                      # unit but nothing to verify against
    assert answers_match("1 kN", 1000.0, expected_unit="N")
    assert not answers_match("1 m", 1000.0, expected_unit="N")      # wrong dimension is never a match


def test_self_healing_uses_the_real_question_not_an_integral_template():
    req = SolverRequest(request_id="s", solver="physics_force", extra_params={"mass": 2, "acceleration": 3})
    mock = ScriptedMockModelClient(["6"])
    res = SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher(), model_client=mock, max_attempts=1).solve(
        req, initial_model_answer="5", question="Force on a 2 kg mass accelerating at 3 m/s^2?")
    assert res.status == "verified"
    assert mock.calls[0]["question"] == "Force on a 2 kg mass accelerating at 3 m/s^2?"
    mock2 = ScriptedMockModelClient(["6"])
    SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher(), model_client=mock2, max_attempts=1).solve(req, initial_model_answer="5")
    assert "physics_force" in mock2.calls[0]["question"] and "integrated" not in mock2.calls[0]["question"]


def test_self_healing_accepts_equivalent_unit_answers():
    req = SolverRequest(request_id="s", solver="physics_force", extra_params={"mass": "2 kg", "acceleration": "3 m/s²"})
    assert SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher()).solve(req, initial_model_answer="0.006 kN").status == "verified"


def test_self_healing_does_not_verify_when_preflight_fails():
    req = SolverRequest(request_id="s", solver="physics_force", extra_params={"mass": "2 m", "acceleration": 3})
    res = SelfHealingSolver(SolverDispatcher()).solve(req, initial_model_answer="6")
    assert res.status == "failed" and "dimension" in res.solver_result.error


# ---------------- interpreter / API ---------------------------------------------
def test_interpreter_keeps_units_and_does_not_crash():
    p = interpret_question("Use Newton second law: mass 500 g and acceleration 2 m/s2, calculate force")
    assert p["solver"] == "physics_force" and p["extra_params"]["mass"] == "500 g"
    assert interpret_question("calculate the reynolds number with density of . velocity of 1 length of 1 viscosity of 1")["status"] == "needs_clarification"
    assert interpret_question("convert 1 MV to V")["extra_params"]["from_unit"] == "MV"      # case preserved
    assert interpret_question("Solve x² − 5x + 6 = 0")["solver"] == "solve_equation"


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def client():
    return TestClient(app, raise_server_exceptions=False)


def test_api_interpret_end_to_end(client):
    r = client.post("/api/interpret", json={"question": "Use Newton second law: mass 500 g and acceleration 2 m/s2, calculate force"})
    assert r.status_code == 200 and r.json()["solver_response"]["numeric_result"] == pytest.approx(1.0)
    r = client.post("/api/interpret", json={"question": "Solve x² − 5x + 6 = 0"})
    assert set(r.json()["solver_response"]["result"]) == {"2", "3"}
    r = client.post("/api/interpret", json={"question": "convert 5 kg to m"})
    assert r.json()["solver_response"]["status"] == "failed"


def test_api_dispatch_preflight_errors_are_structured(client):
    r = client.post("/api/solvers/dispatch", json={"request_id": "a", "solver": "physics_force", "extra_params": {"mass": 3}})
    body = r.json()
    assert r.status_code == 200 and body["status"] == "failed"
    assert body["details"]["preflight"]["issues"][0] == {"category": "missing", "code": "missing_input", "field": "acceleration",
                                                         "message": "Required input 'acceleration' is missing (expected acceleration in m/s^2)"}


def test_api_solve_passes_the_question_through(client):
    r = client.post("/api/solve", json={"question": "force?", "model_answer": "6",
                                         "solver_request": {"request_id": "a", "solver": "physics_force", "extra_params": {"mass": 2, "acceleration": 3}}})
    assert r.json()["status"] == "verified"


def test_practice_grading_cannot_be_bypassed_with_huge_tolerance(client):
    assert client.post("/api/practice/grade", json={"question_id": "integral-2", "submitted_value": 0, "tolerance": 1}).status_code == 422
    r = client.post("/api/practice/grade", json={"question_id": "integral-2", "submitted_value": 0, "tolerance": 0.05})
    assert r.json()["correct"] is False
    assert client.post("/api/practice/grade", json={"question_id": "integral-2", "submitted_value": 9}).json()["correct"] is True
    assert client.post("/api/practice/grade", json={"question_id": "nope", "submitted_value": 9}).status_code == 404
    assert client.post("/api/practice/grade", content='{"question_id":"integral-2","submitted_value":NaN}',
                       headers={"content-type": "application/json"}).status_code == 422


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_input_validation.py ---

import math
import pytest
from app.inputs.dimensions import infer_dimension
from app.inputs.specs import DOMAIN_SPECS, MATH_SPECS, verify_spec
from app.inputs.units import ENERGY, FORCE, LENGTH, MASS, SPEED, DimensionError, parse_unit
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.dispatcher import SolverDispatcher


def run(solver, **kw):
    return SolverDispatcher().dispatch(SolverRequest(request_id="v", solver=solver, **kw))


def cats(r):
    return {i["category"] for i in r.details["preflight"]["issues"]}


def codes(r):
    return {i["code"] for i in r.details["preflight"]["issues"]}


# ---------------- variables --------------------------------------------------
def test_undeclared_variable_is_named_and_distinguished():
    r = run("solve_equation", expression="y + 2 = 5")
    assert r.status == "failed" and "y" in r.error and codes(r) == {"undeclared_variable"}
    ok = run("solve_equation", expression="y + 2 = 5", variable="y")
    assert ok.result == ["3"]
    r = run("differentiate", expression="a*x", variable="x")
    assert "a" in r.error
    assert run("differentiate", expression="a*x", variable="x", variables=["a"]).result == "a"


def test_unknown_function_and_syntax_are_rejected():
    assert "Unsupported function" in run("evaluate", expression="foo(2)").error
    assert run("evaluate", expression="__import__('os')").status == "failed"
    assert run("evaluate", expression="(1).__class__").status == "failed"
    assert "parentheses" in run("evaluate", expression="sin 2").error
    assert "argument" in run("evaluate", expression="sin(1,2)").error


def test_variable_must_appear_when_solving():
    r = run("solve_equation", expression="2 + 2 = 4")
    assert r.status == "failed" and codes(r) == {"variable_not_in_expression"}


def test_bad_variable_names():
    assert run("evaluate", expression="1", variables=["sin"]).status == "failed"
    assert run("evaluate", expression="1", variables=["x y"]).status == "failed"


def test_evaluate_missing_value_vs_invalid():
    r = run("evaluate", expression="2*x")
    assert r.status == "failed" and cats(r) == {"missing"} and codes(r) == {"missing_value"}
    r = run("evaluate", expression="2*x", extra_params={"substitutions": {"x": "abc"}})
    assert r.status == "failed" and cats(r) == {"invalid"}
    assert run("evaluate", expression="2*x", extra_params={"substitutions": {"x": 4}}).numeric_result == 8


# ---------------- missing vs invalid ---------------------------------------
def test_missing_versus_invalid_inputs():
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": 10})
    assert cats(r) == {"missing"} and "acceleration" in r.error and "Missing input" in r.error
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": -1, "acceleration": 1})
    assert cats(r) == {"invalid"} and "Invalid input" in r.error
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": None, "acceleration": 1})
    assert cats(r) == {"missing"}
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": "", "acceleration": 1})
    assert cats(r) == {"missing"}
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": -1})
    assert cats(r) == {"missing", "invalid"}


@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", [True, "abc", float("inf"), [1], {"v": 1}])
def test_invalid_types_rejected(bad):
    r = run("physics_force", extra_params={"mass": bad, "acceleration": 1})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "invalid" in cats(r)


def test_physical_constraints():
    assert run("chemistry_ph", extra_params={"concentration": 0}).status == "failed"
    assert run("chemistry_ph", extra_params={"concentration": "1 mmol/L"}).numeric_result == pytest.approx(3)
    assert run("physics_ideal_gas", extra_params={"pressure": 1, "volume": 1, "moles": 1, "temperature": -5}).status == "failed"
    assert run("physics_ideal_gas", extra_params={"pressure": 1, "volume": 1, "moles": 1, "temperature": "-300 °C"}).status == "failed"
    assert run("engineering_reynolds", extra_params={"density": -1, "velocity": 1, "characteristic_length": 1, "dynamic_viscosity": 1}).status == "failed"
    assert run("physics_work", extra_params={"force": 10, "displacement": 2, "angle_degrees": "60 °"}).numeric_result == pytest.approx(10)


def test_numeric_bounds_for_math():
    assert run("binomial_probability", extra_params={"n": 2.5, "k": 1, "p": 0.5}).status == "failed"
    assert run("binomial_probability", extra_params={"n": 4, "k": 5, "p": 0.5}).status == "failed"
    assert run("binomial_probability", extra_params={"n": 4, "k": 2, "p": 1.5}).status == "failed"
    assert run("z_score", extra_params={"value": 1, "mean": 0, "standard_deviation": 0}).status == "failed"
    assert run("percentile", data=[1.0, 2.0], extra_params={"percentile": 101}).status == "failed"
    assert run("bisection_root", expression="x", lower_bound=2, upper_bound=1).status == "failed"
    assert cats(run("bisection_root", expression="x", lower_bound=1)) == {"missing"}
    assert run("variance", data=[1.0], extra_params={"ddof": 1}).status == "failed"
    assert run("sequence_sum", expression="x", extra_params={"start": 5, "stop": 1}).status == "failed"
    assert run("linear_regression", extra_params={"x_values": [1, 1, 1], "y_values": [1, 2, 3]}).status == "failed"
    assert run("physics_kinematics", extra_params={"initial_velocity": 0}).status == "failed"
    assert run("physics_ohms_law", extra_params={"voltage": 1, "current": 1, "resistance": 1}).status == "failed"


# ---------------- dimensional consistency --------------------------------------
def test_adding_mass_to_distance_is_detected():
    r = run("evaluate", expression="m + d", variables=["m", "d"], extra_params={"substitutions": {"m": "5 kg", "d": "3 m"}})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "Dimensional mismatch" in r.error and "mass" in r.error and "length" in r.error
    r = run("simplify", expression="m + d - d", variables=["m", "d"], extra_params={"units": {"m": "kg", "d": "m"}})
    assert r.status == "failed" and codes(r) == {"dimension_mismatch"}       # even though d cancels


def test_equation_sides_must_match():
    r = run("solve_equation", expression="F = m*v", variable="F", variables=["m", "v"],
            extra_params={"units": {"F": "N", "m": "kg", "v": "m/s"}})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "left side" in r.error
    r = run("solve_equation", expression="F = m*a", variable="F", variables=["m", "a"],
            extra_params={"units": {"F": "N", "m": "kg", "a": "m/s^2"}})
    assert r.status == "success" and r.result == ["a*m"]
    assert r.details["preflight"]["dimension"]["equation"].startswith("force")


def test_result_dimension_is_verified():
    kw = dict(expression="m*a", variables=["m", "a"], extra_params={"substitutions": {"m": "2 kg", "a": "3 m/s²"}})
    r = run("evaluate", **kw)
    assert r.numeric_result == 6 and r.details["preflight"]["dimension"]["result"].startswith("force")
    kw["extra_params"] = {**kw["extra_params"], "expected_unit": "N"}
    assert run("evaluate", **kw).status == "success"
    kw["extra_params"]["expected_unit"] = "J"
    r = run("evaluate", **kw)
    assert r.status == "failed" and "expected_unit" in r.error


def test_missing_unit_is_a_missing_input():
    # plain-number substitutions are dimensionless, so m*a is accepted when only m has a unit
    r = run("evaluate", expression="m*a", variables=["m", "a"], extra_params={"units": {"m": "kg"}, "substitutions": {"m": 1, "a": 1}})
    assert r.status == "success"
    r = run("simplify", expression="m*a", variables=["m", "a"], extra_params={"units": {"m": "kg"}})
    assert r.status == "failed" and cats(r) == {"missing"} and codes(r) == {"missing_unit"}


def test_transcendental_functions_need_dimensionless_arguments():
    r = run("simplify", expression="sin(d)", variable="d", extra_params={"units": {"d": "m"}})
    assert r.status == "failed" and "dimensionless" in r.error
    assert run("simplify", expression="exp(d/L)", variable="d", variables=["L"], extra_params={"units": {"d": "m", "L": "m"}}).status == "success"


def test_derivative_and_integral_dimensions():
    r = run("differentiate", expression="s", variable="t", variables=["s"], extra_params={"units": {"s": "m", "t": "s"}, "expected_unit": "m/s"})
    assert r.status == "success"
    r = run("differentiate", expression="s", variable="t", variables=["s"], extra_params={"units": {"s": "m", "t": "s"}, "expected_unit": "m"})
    assert r.status == "failed"


def test_dimension_algebra():
    d = {"m": MASS, "v": SPEED}
    assert infer_dimension("0.5*m*v**2", d) == ENERGY
    assert infer_dimension("sqrt(L)", {"L": LENGTH}) == LENGTH ** __import__("fractions").Fraction(1, 2)
    with pytest.raises(DimensionError):
        infer_dimension("m + v", d)
    with pytest.raises(DimensionError):
        infer_dimension("2**m", d)
    assert infer_dimension("m*a", {"m": MASS, "a": SPEED / parse_unit("s").dim}) == FORCE


def test_every_solver_spec_is_dimensionally_consistent():
    problems = [p for op, spec in {**DOMAIN_SPECS, **MATH_SPECS}.items() for p in verify_spec(op, spec)]
    assert problems == []


# ---------------- numerical residuals ---------------------------------------
def test_numerical_residuals_are_reported():
    r = run("solve_equation", expression="x^3 - 2x - 5 = 0")
    assert r.verification.passed and r.details["symbolic_residuals"]
    r = run("newton_root", expression="x² − 2", initial_guess=1.0)
    assert r.numeric_result == pytest.approx(math.sqrt(2)) and r.details["residual"] < 1e-7
    r = run("solve_system", equations=["x + y = 3", "x − y = 1"], variables=["x", "y"])
    assert r.verification.passed and r.result == [{"x": "2", "y": "1"}]
    r = run("solve_linear_system", matrix=[[2, 1], [1, 3]], vector=[3, 5])
    assert r.details["residual_norm"] < 1e-12
    r = run("matrix_inverse", matrix=[[1, 2], [2, 4.0000000001]])
    assert not r.verification.passed and "ill-conditioned" in r.verification.message


def test_underdetermined_system_is_flagged():
    r = run("solve_system", equations=["x + y = 3"], variables=["x", "y"])
    assert any("underdetermined" in n for n in r.details["preflight"]["notes"])

import pytest
from pydantic import ValidationError
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest


def test_valid_request_defaults_variable():
    request = SolverRequest(request_id="test-1", solver="definite_integral", expression="x**2", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=3)
    assert request.variable == "x"


def test_rejects_unknown_solver():
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        SolverRequest(request_id="test-2", solver="made_up", expression="x", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=1)


def test_rejects_extra_fields():
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        SolverRequest(request_id="test-3", solver="definite_integral", expression="x", lower_bound=0, upper_bound=1, command="bad")


def test_rejects_non_numeric_bound():
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        SolverRequest(request_id="test-4", solver="definite_integral", expression="x", lower_bound="zero", upper_bound=1)


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_integration.py ---

import pytest
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.integration import DefiniteIntegralSolver


def request(expr, lower=0, upper=3):
    return SolverRequest(request_id="test-int", solver="definite_integral", expression=expr, lower_bound=lower, upper_bound=upper)


def test_integrates_x_squared():
    result = DefiniteIntegralSolver().solve(request("x**2"))
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert result.exact_result == "9"
    assert result.numeric_result == pytest.approx(9.0)
    assert result.verification.passed


def test_integrates_constant():
    result = DefiniteIntegralSolver().solve(request("5", 0, 2))
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert result.exact_result == "10"


def test_integrates_sine():
    result = DefiniteIntegralSolver().solve(request("sin(x)", 0, 1))
    assert result.status == "success"
    assert result.numeric_result == pytest.approx(1 - __import__("math").cos(1))


def test_rejects_reversed_bounds():
    result = DefiniteIntegralSolver().solve(request("x**2", 3, 0))
    assert result.status == "failed"
    assert "Lower bound" in result.error


def test_rejects_unsafe_expression():
    result = DefiniteIntegralSolver().solve(request("__import__('os')"))
    assert result.status == "failed"


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/tests/test_domain_solvers.py ---

import math
from app.schemas.models import SolverRequest
from app.solvers.dispatcher import SolverDispatcher
from app.solvers.domain_stem import interpret_question, convert_units


def run(solver, **kwargs):
    return SolverDispatcher().dispatch(SolverRequest(request_id='domain-test', solver=solver, **kwargs))


def test_physics_force_energy_and_ohms_law():
    assert run('physics_force', extra_params={'mass': 10, 'acceleration': 2}).numeric_result == 20
    assert run('physics_kinetic_energy', extra_params={'mass': 2, 'velocity': 3}).numeric_result == 9
    r = run('physics_ohms_law', extra_params={'voltage': 12, 'resistance': 4})
    assert r.status == 'success' and r.result['value'] == 3


def test_unit_conversions_and_dimension_validation():
    assert abs(convert_units(100, 'km/h', 'm/s')['value'] - 27.7777777778) < 1e-8
    assert abs(convert_units(0, 'celsius', 'fahrenheit')['value'] - 32) < 1e-9
    r = run('unit_convert', extra_params={'value': 1, 'from_unit': 'kg', 'to_unit': 'm'})
    assert r.status == 'failed' and 'Incompatible units' in r.error


def test_chemistry_molar_mass_and_balancing():
    r = run('chemistry_molar_mass', extra_params={'formula': 'H2O'})
    assert r.status == 'success' and abs(r.result['molar_mass_g_mol'] - 18.015) < .01
    r = run('chemistry_balance_equation', extra_params={'equation': 'H2 + O2 -> H2O'})
    assert r.status == 'success'
    assert r.result['balanced_equation'] == '2H2 + O2 -> 2H2O'


def test_chemistry_concentration_and_ph():
    assert run('chemistry_molarity', extra_params={'moles': .5, 'volume_liters': 2}).numeric_result == .25
    assert abs(run('chemistry_ph', extra_params={'concentration': 1e-3}).numeric_result - 3) < 1e-12


def test_engineering_calculations_and_constraints():
    r=run('engineering_reynolds', extra_params={'density': 1000, 'velocity': 1, 'characteristic_length': .1, 'dynamic_viscosity': .001})
    assert r.status == 'success' and r.numeric_result == 100000
    r=run('engineering_voltage_divider', extra_params={'input_voltage': 12, 'r1': 1000, 'r2': 1000})
    assert r.numeric_result == 6
    r=run('engineering_safety_factor', extra_params={'failure_strength': 300, 'working_stress': 100})
    assert r.numeric_result == 3
    assert run('engineering_reynolds', extra_params={'density': -1, 'velocity': 1, 'characteristic_length': 1, 'dynamic_viscosity': 1}).status == 'failed'


def test_natural_language_interpreter_supported_and_unsupported_prompts():
    p=interpret_question('Use Newton second law: mass 10 kg and acceleration 2 m/s2, calculate force')
    assert p['solver'] == 'physics_force'
    p=interpret_question('Molar mass of H2O')
    assert p['solver'] == 'chemistry_molar_mass' and p['extra_params']['formula'] == 'H2O'
    p=interpret_question('What is the weather?')
    assert p['status'] == 'needs_clarification'


def test_new_math_notation_normalization():
    r=run('solve_equation', expression='x² − 5x + 6 = 0', variable='x')
    assert r.status == 'success' and set(r.result) == {'2','3'}


def test_expression_parser_common_notation_and_functions():
    assert run('evaluate', expression='sqrt(9) + 2x', variable='x', extra_params={}).status == 'failed'  # evaluate requires a bound x
    r=run('evaluate', expression='sqrt(9) + 2*3')
    assert r.status == 'success' and r.numeric_result == 9



--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/app/__init__.py ---



--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/app/inputs/specs.py ---

"""Per-operation input specifications for the preflight layer.

Plain numbers are interpreted in the unit listed here (SI unless noted), exactly as the
solvers always did; quantities with units are converted into it.  The ``formula`` /
``equations`` / ``alternatives`` entries are checked for dimensional consistency by the
test-suite (and can be re-checked at runtime with :func:`verify_spec`).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Callable

from app.inputs.constraints import Issue, ParamSpec, invalid, missing
from app.inputs.dimensions import infer_dimension
from app.inputs.units import (ACCELERATION, DIMENSIONLESS, ENERGY, FORCE, LENGTH, MOMENTUM, POWER,
                              PRESSURE, SPEED, TIME, Dimension, parse_unit)

Rule = Callable[[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any], list[Issue]], None]   # (values, raw_extra, issues)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class OpSpec:
    params: tuple[ParamSpec, ...] = ()
    top_required: tuple[str, ...] = ()               # SolverRequest fields that must be present
    output_unit: str | None = None                   # unit of a scalar result
    formula: str | None = None                       # dimension of this formula must equal output_unit
    equations: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = ()      # both sides must have equal dimension
    alternatives: tuple[tuple[str, str, str], ...] = ()   # (target, formula, unit) for multi-target ops
    rules: tuple[Rule, ...] = ()
    same_units: tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...] = ()     # if one is given with a unit, all must be
    description: str = ""


def N(name, unit=None, **kw) -> ParamSpec:          # noqa: N802 - terse constructor
    return ParamSpec(name, "number", unit, **kw)


def I(name, **kw) -> ParamSpec:                       # noqa: E743
    return ParamSpec(name, "integer", None, **kw)


def L(name, **kw) -> ParamSpec:
    return ParamSpec(name, "number_list", None, **kw)


POS = {"min": 0, "min_open": True}
NONNEG = {"min": 0}
OPT = {"required": False}


def _provided(values: dict[str, Any], names: tuple[str, ...]) -> list[str]:
    return [n for n in names if values.get(n) is not None]


def _rule_ohm(values, raw, issues):
    given = _provided(values, ("voltage", "current", "resistance"))
    if len(given) < 2:
        issues.append(missing("voltage/current/resistance", "Provide exactly two of voltage, current, resistance"))
    elif len(given) == 3:
        issues.append(invalid("voltage/current/resistance", "Provide exactly two of voltage, current, resistance; "
                                                          "all three were given", "overspecified"))


def _rule_power(values, raw, issues):
    given = _provided(values, ("voltage", "current", "resistance"))
    if len(given) < 2:
        issues.append(missing("voltage/current/resistance",
                              "Provide voltage and current, current and resistance, or voltage and resistance"))
    elif len(given) == 3:
        v, i, r = values["voltage"], values["current"], values["resistance"]
        if abs(v - i * r) > 1e-6 * max(1.0, abs(v), abs(i * r)):
            issues.append(invalid("voltage/current/resistance",
                                  f"voltage ({v:g}) does not equal current*resistance ({i * r:g}); inputs are inconsistent",
                                  "inconsistent_inputs"))


_KIN = ("initial_velocity", "final_velocity", "acceleration", "time", "displacement")


def _rule_kinematics(values, raw, issues):
    given = _provided(values, _KIN)
    if len(given) < 3:
        issues.append(missing("kinematics", "Provide at least three of initial_velocity, final_velocity, "
                                            "acceleration, time, displacement"))
    elif len(given) == len(_KIN):
        issues.append(invalid("kinematics", "All five kinematics quantities were given; omit the one to solve for",
                              "overspecified"))
    target = raw.get("target")
    if target is not None and target not in _KIN:
        issues.append(invalid("target", f"target must be one of {', '.join(_KIN)}", "invalid_choice"))
    if target in _KIN and values.get(target) is not None:
        issues.append(invalid("target", f"target '{target}' was also supplied as an input", "overspecified"))


KINEMATICS_ALT = (
    ("final_velocity", "initial_velocity+acceleration*time", "m/s"),
    ("initial_velocity", "final_velocity-acceleration*time", "m/s"),
    ("acceleration", "(final_velocity-initial_velocity)/time", "m/s^2"),
    ("time", "(final_velocity-initial_velocity)/acceleration", "s"),
    ("displacement", "initial_velocity*time+0.5*acceleration*time**2", "m"),
)

G = N("gravity", "m/s^2", required=False, **POS, constraint="gravitational acceleration is positive")

DOMAIN_SPECS: dict[str, OpSpec] = {
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ physics
    "physics_force": OpSpec(
        (N("mass", "kg", **POS, constraint="mass is positive"), N("acceleration", "m/s^2")),
        output_unit="N", formula="mass*acceleration"),
    "physics_weight": OpSpec(
        (N("mass", "kg", **POS, constraint="mass is positive"), G),
        output_unit="N", formula="mass*gravity"),
    "physics_kinetic_energy": OpSpec(
        (N("mass", "kg", **POS, constraint="mass is positive"), N("velocity", "m/s")),
        output_unit="J", formula="0.5*mass*velocity**2"),
    "physics_momentum": OpSpec(
        (N("mass", "kg", **POS, constraint="mass is positive"), N("velocity", "m/s")),
        output_unit="kg*m/s", formula="mass*velocity"),
    "physics_potential_energy": OpSpec(
        (N("mass", "kg", **POS, constraint="mass is positive"), N("height", "m"), G),
        output_unit="J", formula="mass*gravity*height"),
    "physics_density": OpSpec(
        (N("mass", "kg", **NONNEG, constraint="mass cannot be negative"),
         N("volume", "m^3", **POS, constraint="volume is positive")),
        output_unit="kg/m^3", formula="mass/volume"),
    "physics_pressure": OpSpec(
        (N("force", "N"), N("area", "m^2", **POS, constraint="area is positive")),
        output_unit="Pa", formula="force/area"),
    "physics_ohms_law": OpSpec(
        (N("voltage", "V", required=False), N("current", "A", required=False),
         N("resistance", "ohm", required=False, **NONNEG, constraint="resistance cannot be negative")),
        alternatives=(("voltage", "current*resistance", "V"), ("current", "voltage/resistance", "A"),
                      ("resistance", "voltage/current", "ohm")),
        rules=(_rule_ohm,)),
    "physics_electrical_power": OpSpec(
        (N("voltage", "V", required=False), N("current", "A", required=False),
         N("resistance", "ohm", required=False, **NONNEG, constraint="resistance cannot be negative")),
        output_unit="W",
        alternatives=(("P=VI", "voltage*current", "W"), ("P=I2R", "current**2*resistance", "W"),
                      ("P=V2/R", "voltage**2/resistance", "W")),
        rules=(_rule_power,)),
    "physics_wave_speed": OpSpec(
        (N("frequency", "Hz", **POS, constraint="frequency is positive"),
         N("wavelength", "m", **POS, constraint="wavelength is positive")),
        output_unit="m/s", formula="frequency*wavelength"),
    "physics_ideal_gas": OpSpec(
        (N("pressure", "Pa", **NONNEG, constraint="absolute pressure cannot be negative"),
         N("volume", "m^3", **POS, constraint="volume is positive"),
         N("moles", "mol", **POS, constraint="amount of substance is positive"),
         N("temperature", "K", **POS, constraint="absolute temperature is positive"),
         N("gas_constant", "J/(mol*K)", required=False, **POS)),
        equations=(("pressure*volume", "moles*gas_constant*temperature"),)),
    "physics_work": OpSpec(
        (N("force", "N"), N("displacement", "m"), N("angle_degrees", "deg", required=False)),
        output_unit="J", formula="force*displacement"),
    "physics_hydrostatic_pressure": OpSpec(
        (N("density", "kg/m^3", **POS, constraint="density is positive"),
         N("depth", "m", **NONNEG, constraint="depth cannot be negative"), G),
        output_unit="Pa", formula="density*gravity*depth"),
    "physics_heat_energy": OpSpec(
        (N("mass", "kg", **POS, constraint="mass is positive"),
         N("specific_heat", "J/(kg*K)", **POS, constraint="specific heat is positive"),
         N("temperature_change", "K", temperature="delta")),
        output_unit="J", formula="mass*specific_heat*temperature_change"),
    "physics_kinematics": OpSpec(
        (N("initial_velocity", "m/s", required=False), N("final_velocity", "m/s", required=False),
         N("acceleration", "m/s^2", required=False),
         N("time", "s", required=False, **NONNEG, constraint="time cannot be negative"),
         N("displacement", "m", required=False)),
        alternatives=KINEMATICS_ALT, rules=(_rule_kinematics,)),
    # ---------------------------------------------------------------- chemistry
    "chemistry_molarity": OpSpec(
        (N("moles", "mol", **NONNEG, constraint="amount of substance cannot be negative"),
         N("volume_liters", "L", **POS, constraint="volume is positive")),
        output_unit="mol/L", formula="moles/volume_liters"),
    "chemistry_dilution": OpSpec(
        (N("initial_concentration", "mol/L", **NONNEG, constraint="concentration cannot be negative"),
         N("initial_volume", "L", **NONNEG, constraint="volume cannot be negative"),
         N("final_volume", "L", **POS, constraint="volume is positive")),
        output_unit="mol/L", formula="initial_concentration*initial_volume/final_volume"),
    "chemistry_ideal_gas": OpSpec(
        (N("pressure", "atm", **NONNEG, constraint="absolute pressure cannot be negative"),
         N("volume", "L", **POS, constraint="volume is positive"),
         N("temperature", "K", **POS, constraint="absolute temperature is positive"),
         N("gas_constant", "L*atm/(mol*K)", required=False, **POS)),
        output_unit="mol", formula="pressure*volume/(gas_constant*temperature)"),
    "chemistry_ph": OpSpec(

        formula=None),
    "chemistry_stoichiometry": OpSpec(

        output_unit="mol", formula="given_moles*target_coefficient/given_coefficient"),
    # -------------------------------------------------------------- engineering
    "engineering_reynolds": OpSpec(

    "engineering_efficiency": OpSpec(
        (N("useful_output", **NONNEG, constraint="useful output cannot be negative"),
         N("total_input", **POS, constraint="input is positive")),
        output_unit="1"),
    "engineering_voltage_divider": OpSpec(

        output_unit="V", formula="input_voltage*r2/(r1+r2)"),
    "engineering_bending_stress": OpSpec(
        (N("bending_moment", "N*m"), N("second_moment_area", "m^4", **POS, constraint="second moment of area is positive"),
         N("distance_from_neutral_axis", "m")),
        output_unit="Pa", formula="bending_moment*distance_from_neutral_axis/second_moment_area"),
    "engineering_flow_rate": OpSpec(
        (N("area", "m^2", **NONNEG, constraint="area cannot be negative"), N("average_velocity", "m/s")),
        output_unit="m^3/s", formula="area*average_velocity"),
    "engineering_safety_factor": OpSpec(
        (N("failure_strength", "Pa", **POS), N("working_stress", "Pa", **POS)),

        same_units=(("failure_strength", "working_stress"),)),
}

# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Core mathematics: which request fields / extra_params each operation needs
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
def _rule_sequence(values, raw, issues):
    start, stop = values.get("start", 1), values.get("stop", 10)
    if start > stop:
        issues.append(invalid("start/stop", f"start ({start}) must not exceed stop ({stop})", "out_of_range"))
    elif stop - start > 100_000:
        issues.append(invalid("start/stop", "Sequence range is too large (limit 100000 terms)", "out_of_range"))


def _rule_regression(values, raw, issues):
    xs, ys = values.get("x_values"), values.get("y_values")
    if xs is None or ys is None:
        return
    if len(xs) != len(ys):
        issues.append(invalid("x_values/y_values", f"x_values ({len(xs)}) and y_values ({len(ys)}) must have equal length", "length_mismatch"))
    elif len(set(xs)) < 2:
        issues.append(invalid("x_values", "x_values are all identical, so a regression/correlation is undefined", "degenerate_data"))


def _rule_interpolate(values, raw, issues):
    xs, ys = values.get("x_values"), values.get("y_values")
    if xs is not None and ys is not None and len(xs) != len(ys):
        issues.append(invalid("x_values/y_values", f"x_values ({len(xs)}) and y_values ({len(ys)}) must have equal length", "length_mismatch"))


MATH_SPECS: dict[str, OpSpec] = {

def get_spec(op: str) -> OpSpec | None:
    return DOMAIN_SPECS.get(op) or MATH_SPECS.get(op)


def verify_spec(op: str, spec: OpSpec) -> list[str]:
    """Return dimensional problems found in a spec's formulas (empty list = consistent)."""
    problems: list[str] = []
    dims = {p.name: p.dim for p in spec.params}
    if spec.formula and spec.output_unit:
        try:
            got, want = infer_dimension(spec.formula, dims), parse_unit(spec.output_unit).dim if spec.output_unit != "1" else DIMENSIONLESS
            if got != want:
                problems.append(f"{op}: formula {spec.formula!r} has dimension {got}, output unit {spec.output_unit!r} is {want}")
        except Exception as exc:                                   # noqa: BLE001
            problems.append(f"{op}: {exc}")
    for lhs, rhs in spec.equations:
        try:
            a, b = infer_dimension(lhs, dims), infer_dimension(rhs, dims)
            if a != b:
                problems.append(f"{op}: {lhs!r} ({a}) != {rhs!r} ({b})")
        except Exception as exc:                                   # noqa: BLE001
            problems.append(f"{op}: {exc}")
    for target, formula, unit in spec.alternatives:
        try:
            got, want = infer_dimension(formula, dims), parse_unit(unit).dim
            if got != want:
                problems.append(f"{op}/{target}: {formula!r} has dimension {got}, expected {want}")
        except Exception as exc:                                   # noqa: BLE001
            problems.append(f"{op}/{target}: {exc}")
    return problems


--- STEM-Matrix-main/stemmatrix-unix/backend/app/inputs/units.py ---

"""Scientific unit parser, quantities and dimensional vectors.

Quantities such as ``500 g``, ``2 m/s²``, ``25 °C`` or ``1.5×10⁻³ kg·m/s^2`` are parsed
into a value plus a :class:`Unit` (a dimension vector, an SI scale factor and, for
°C/°F, an offset).  Converting between incompatible dimensions raises
:class:`DimensionError` instead of silently calculating.

SI prefixes are case-sensitive (``mV`` = millivolt, ``MV`` = megavolt, ``Mm`` =
megametre).  Only when a token has no exact reading is a case-insensitive reading
tried, and that is reported in ``notes`` so it is never silent.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import difflib
import math
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from fractions import Fraction
from typing import Iterable

from app.inputs.notation import NotationError, convert_superscripts, split_number_prefix

BASE_SYMBOLS = ("L", "M", "T", "I", "Θ", "N")      # length, mass, time, current, temperature, amount


class UnitError(ValueError):
    """A unit or quantity could not be interpreted."""


class DimensionError(UnitError):
    """Two quantities have incompatible physical dimensions."""


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Dimensions
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Dimension:
    exps: tuple[Fraction, ...] = (Fraction(0),) * 6

    @staticmethod
    def of(L=0, M=0, T=0, I=0, Th=0, N=0) -> "Dimension":          # noqa: E741
        return Dimension(tuple(Fraction(v) for v in (L, M, T, I, Th, N)))

    def __mul__(self, other: "Dimension") -> "Dimension":
        return Dimension(tuple(a + b for a, b in zip(self.exps, other.exps)))

    def __truediv__(self, other: "Dimension") -> "Dimension":
        return Dimension(tuple(a - b for a, b in zip(self.exps, other.exps)))

    def __pow__(self, power) -> "Dimension":
        p = Fraction(power)
        return Dimension(tuple(a * p for a in self.exps))

    @property
    def dimensionless(self) -> bool:
        return all(a == 0 for a in self.exps)

    def formula(self) -> str:
        if self.dimensionless:
            return "dimensionless"
        parts = []
        for sym, e in zip(BASE_SYMBOLS, self.exps):
            if e == 0:
                continue
            parts.append(sym if e == 1 else f"{sym}^{e if e.denominator == 1 else f'({e})'}")
        return "·".join(parts)

    def name(self) -> str:
        return _NAMED.get(self, self.formula())

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        n = _NAMED.get(self)
        return f"{n} [{self.formula()}]" if n and not self.dimensionless else self.formula()


D = Dimension.of
DIMENSIONLESS = D()
LENGTH, MASS, TIME, CURRENT, TEMPERATURE, AMOUNT = D(L=1), D(M=1), D(T=1), D(I=1), D(Th=1), D(N=1)
AREA, VOLUME = LENGTH ** 2, LENGTH ** 3
SPEED, ACCELERATION = LENGTH / TIME, LENGTH / TIME ** 2
FORCE = MASS * ACCELERATION
ENERGY, POWER = FORCE * LENGTH, FORCE * LENGTH / TIME
PRESSURE = FORCE / AREA
MOMENTUM = MASS * SPEED
CHARGE = CURRENT * TIME
VOLTAGE = POWER / CURRENT
RESISTANCE = VOLTAGE / CURRENT
CAPACITANCE = CHARGE / VOLTAGE
DENSITY = MASS / VOLUME
CONCENTRATION = AMOUNT / VOLUME
FLOW_RATE = VOLUME / TIME
FREQUENCY = TIME ** -1
DYNAMIC_VISCOSITY = PRESSURE * TIME
SPECIFIC_HEAT = ENERGY / MASS / TEMPERATURE
HEAT_TRANSFER_COEFF = POWER / AREA / TEMPERATURE
MOLAR_MASS = MASS / AMOUNT
GAS_CONSTANT = ENERGY / AMOUNT / TEMPERATURE
SECOND_MOMENT_AREA = LENGTH ** 4
CONDUCTANCE = RESISTANCE ** -1
MAGNETIC_FLUX_DENSITY = MASS / CURRENT / TIME ** 2
INDUCTANCE = VOLTAGE * TIME / CURRENT

_NAMED: dict[Dimension, str] = {
    DIMENSIONLESS: "dimensionless", LENGTH: "length", MASS: "mass", TIME: "time", CURRENT: "current",
    TEMPERATURE: "temperature", AMOUNT: "amount", AREA: "area", VOLUME: "volume", SPEED: "speed",
    ACCELERATION: "acceleration", FORCE: "force", ENERGY: "energy", POWER: "power", PRESSURE: "pressure",
    MOMENTUM: "momentum", CHARGE: "charge", VOLTAGE: "voltage", RESISTANCE: "resistance",
    CAPACITANCE: "capacitance", DENSITY: "density", CONCENTRATION: "concentration", FLOW_RATE: "flow_rate",
    FREQUENCY: "frequency", DYNAMIC_VISCOSITY: "dynamic_viscosity", SPECIFIC_HEAT: "specific_heat",
    HEAT_TRANSFER_COEFF: "heat_transfer_coefficient", MOLAR_MASS: "molar_mass", GAS_CONSTANT: "gas_constant",
    SECOND_MOMENT_AREA: "second_moment_of_area", CONDUCTANCE: "conductance",
    MAGNETIC_FLUX_DENSITY: "magnetic_flux_density", INDUCTANCE: "inductance",
}


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Unit table
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Unit:
    dim: Dimension
    factor: Fraction                 # SI = value * factor + offset
    offset: Fraction = Fraction(0)
    symbol: str = ""

    @property
    def affine(self) -> bool:
        return self.offset != 0

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.symbol


_F = Fraction
# symbol -> (dimension, factor to SI, prefixable)
_BASE: dict[str, tuple[Dimension, Fraction, bool]] = {
    # length
    "m": (LENGTH, _F(1), True), "in": (LENGTH, _F("0.0254"), False), "ft": (LENGTH, _F("0.3048"), False),
    "yd": (LENGTH, _F("0.9144"), False), "mi": (LENGTH, _F("1609.344"), False), "nmi": (LENGTH, _F(1852), False),
    "Å": (LENGTH, _F("1e-10"), False),
    # mass (gram is the prefixable base; kg = k + g)
    "g": (MASS, _F("0.001"), True), "lb": (MASS, _F("0.45359237"), False), "oz": (MASS, _F("0.028349523125"), False),
    "t": (MASS, _F(1000), False),
    # time
    "s": (TIME, _F(1), True), "min": (TIME, _F(60), False), "h": (TIME, _F(3600), False),
    "hr": (TIME, _F(3600), False), "d": (TIME, _F(86400), False),
    # electrical / amount / frequency
    "A": (CURRENT, _F(1), True), "mol": (AMOUNT, _F(1), True), "Hz": (FREQUENCY, _F(1), True),
    "C": (CHARGE, _F(1), True), "V": (VOLTAGE, _F(1), True), "F": (CAPACITANCE, _F(1), True),
    "Ω": (RESISTANCE, _F(1), True), "ohm": (RESISTANCE, _F(1), True),
    # mechanics
    "N": (FORCE, _F(1), True), "lbf": (FORCE, _F("4.4482216152605"), False),
    "kgf": (FORCE, _F("9.80665"), False), "dyn": (FORCE, _F("1e-5"), False),
    "Pa": (PRESSURE, _F(1), True), "bar": (PRESSURE, _F(100000), True), "atm": (PRESSURE, _F(101325), False),
    "psi": (PRESSURE, _F("6894.757293168"), False), "mmHg": (PRESSURE, _F("133.322387415"), False),
    "torr": (PRESSURE, _F(101325) / 760, False),
    "J": (ENERGY, _F(1), True), "cal": (ENERGY, _F("4.184"), True), "eV": (ENERGY, _F("1.602176634e-19"), True),
    "Wh": (ENERGY, _F(3600), True), "BTU": (ENERGY, _F("1055.05585262"), False),
